D1 (Fri Jan 1) The Tower and the Time Capsule
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Cardboard Pile
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The First Book of the Year
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box]
    **🧰 You need:** the recycling pile · 3 paper bags or empty boxes · masking tape.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands]
    - The sorted boxes, tubes and cups
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 measuring tape or a length of string
    - 1 permanent marker
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule]
    - 1 shoebox with a lid
    - Thick card, 2 sheets
    - Washable paint and a wide brush
    - 1 wet washcloth in a shallow bowl
    - 1 pencil, 1 permanent marker
    - 1 measuring tape
    - Packing tape
    - Today's walk treasure
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Handprints only. Skip the box and do the rest tomorrow.
    - Trace round her whole body on the back of a flattened box instead.
    - Sort the walk treasure by color, then by size.
    - Look through last year's photos and name who is in each one.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - A friend's house, if anyone is up for a visitor on New Year's Day
    - The park, emptier today than any other day this year
    - The same walk again in fading light. Ask what is different; the answer is usually the sound
    - Drive somewhere with a view and stay in the warm car
    - Mail a letter to Azlyn for her to open when she is five
D2 (Sat Jan 2) The Box House
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box

    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box]
    **🧰 You need:** the big box · masking tape · 1 permanent marker.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House]
    - 1 large box, tall enough for her to sit up in
    - 1 craft knife or heavy scissors (**adult only**)
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 permanent marker
    - Offcut card and small boxes
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House]
    - The box house
    - 6–10 small boxes, cups and tubes
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 scrap of fabric or an old dish towel
    - 1 permanent marker
    - Crayons or paint
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Throw one blanket over one box and stop there.
    - Draw the house you'd like to live in on a flattened box instead.
    - Post things through the windows and fish them back out.
    - Cut a big cardboard door for the *real* doorway and pretend the house is the room.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **The museum**, first Saturday of the month and the year's first
    - The park, to run off whatever the box didn't
    - Indoor playground if it's raining sideways
    - A café with a window seat and something to look at
    - The hardware store to look at boxes, lumber and things that stack
D3 (Sun Jan 3) Ramps and Bridges
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Bean Jar
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Warm Bath, Long Books
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes]
    **🧰 You need:** cardboard tubes · masking tape · scissors · a smooth wall or the side of a closet.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run]
    - 6–10 cardboard tubes, some cut open
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - Dried beans, dried pasta, a ping-pong ball if you have one
    - 1 shallow box or saucepan for the bottom
    - A wall or closet side you don't mind taping
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy]
    - 2 dining chairs, set a hand's width apart to start
    - Flattened cardboard, several pieces
    - Craft sticks and cardboard tubes
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 teddy or doll
    - A can of beans as the heavy test weight
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Bridge two sofa cushions with one piece of card and stop there.
    - Build a bridge for toy cars instead of teddy.
    - Fold paper into concertinas and stand books on them.
    - Lay a plank between two low stools outside and walk across it.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - A long walk with the stroller, the whole loop, no destination
    - Feed the ducks and count the bridges you cross
    - The park, for the climbing frame she can look at as a structure now
    - A drive to somewhere with a real bridge, and stand under it
    - Indoors if the rain has set in: a book about bridges and a tunnel under the dining table

  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]

    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Rebuilding Alone
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and the Quiet Corner
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids]
    **🧰 You need:** paper cups, 20 or so · a flat bit of floor.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment]
    - Boxes, cups and tubes from the build box
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - Paper and a marker for recording
    - The ball run from yesterday, still taped to the wall
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball]
    - 1 clean sock
    - Dried beans or rice to fill it
    - 1 ball of string, about six feet
    - A door frame, a broom handle across two chairs, or a curtain rail bracket
    - All the boxes and cups
    - Masking tape
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Roll a ball at the towers instead of swinging anything.
    - Knock towers over with a cardboard tube used as a bat.
    - Build and flatten with feet only, no hands.
    - Stack cushions and jump into them.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - The riverside path, to look at what holds the bank up
    - The woods loop, hunting for fallen trees and what pulled them down
    - The neighborhood park
    - The duck pond, and the bridge over it
    - Indoor playground, if it is pouring and nobody can face the coats
D5 (Tue Jan 5) Bricks and Mortar
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Swimming** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Leftover Dough
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and Two Books
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks]
    **🧰 You need:** the salt dough · a rolling pin · a table knife · a floured board.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks]
    - The salt dough, 2 cups flour to 1 cup salt to 1 cup water
    - 1 rolling pin, 1 table knife
    - 1 baking tray and parchment paper
    - Extra flour for the board
    - 1 pencil for scoring
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall]
    - The cooled salt dough bricks, thirty or so
    - A flat tray or baking sheet as the building site
    - 1 small bowl of flour and water paste
    - 1 teaspoon for the paste
    - A ruler or a straight piece of card
    - The teddy from Sunday
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Bricks only, no paste, and knock it down when it bores her.
    - Press leaves and buttons into the leftover dough instead.
    - Paint the dried bricks and build tomorrow.
    - Sort the bricks by size into rows and stop there.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Swimming**]
    - **Swimming**, the weekly one and the first of fifty-two
    - Indoor playground if the pool is shut for the holidays
    - The library, a day early
    - A warm café and a window, if nobody has the energy
    - The indoor play center

    - Tape a string across the bath and float things along it.
  [🌳 Out Again: **The Library**]

    - Indoor playground if the library is packed with the holiday crowd
    - The store, and let her carry one thing
    - The park on the way home
    - A café and the new book, read cover to cover once

D8 (Fri Jan 8) Shakers and Spoons
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Quietest Shaker
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: Bath and the Rhyme Book
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard]
    **🧰 You need:** the dry-goods cupboard, open · 1 metal spoon · 5 empty pots with lids.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set]
    - 5 small plastic bottles or yogurt pots with lids
    - Dried rice, 1 cup
    - Dried lentils, 1 cup
    - Dried pasta shapes, 1 cup
    - Sugar, 2 tablespoons
    - Dry cereal, a handful
    - Packing tape
    - 1 permanent marker
    - 1 sheet of paper rolled into a funnel
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song]
    - The five sealed shakers
    - 1 saucepan and 1 wooden spoon
    - 1 cardboard tube for a megaphone
    - 1 scarf as the conductor's signal
    - Your phone, to record the last run
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Two shakers and one rhyme, sat on the sofa.
    - March round the kitchen table with the saucepan and nothing else.
    - Sort the shakers by what is inside instead of how loud they are.
    - Sing the same rhyme in a squeaky voice, a growly voice and a whisper.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **A cafe with cake**, a window seat and a street to watch
    - The neighborhood park, for twenty minutes of running
    - The bakery counter, and let her choose for tomorrow
    - A friend's house, if anyone can take an afternoon of shakers
    - Home, with the shakers in the bath
D9 (Sat Jan 9) Humpty Dumpty's Wall
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Egg Cups
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Quiet End of a Loud Day
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs]
    **🧰 You need:** 4 cold boiled eggs · 1 permanent marker · 1 egg cup.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record]
    - 4 hard-boiled eggs, boiled last night and cooled, or boiled at 8:00 and cooled fast in a bowl of cold water
    - 1 baking tray
    - 1 old towel to line the tray
    - 6–8 books or small boxes to build the wall
    - 1 permanent marker
    - Paper for the record
    - 1 tape measure
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg]
    - The cracked shells from this morning
    - 1 sheet of thick paper or card
    - A glue stick, or flour and water paste in a saucer
    - 1 teaspoon for the paste
    - Crayons
    - 1 pencil to draw the egg outline
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Shells into a bowl of water and stir. That is the whole activity.
    - Draw Humpty on a flattened box and glue nothing.
    - Paint the shells today and glue them tomorrow.
    - Sort the pieces into big, medium and tiny on three saucers.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **A bus ride to nowhere in particular**, upstairs at the front
    - The park, for the low wall she balanced on this morning
    - Indoor playground if the weather has gone sideways
    - A walk to look at real brick walls and count the courses
    - The store for eggs, since you are four down
D10 (Sun Jan 10) Five Little Ducks
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]

    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: The Forks
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Ducks on the Hill
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: One Last Rhyme from the Bag
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 flattened cereal box · scissors (adult only) · crayons · 5 clothespins.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away]
    - 1 cereal box, for cutting five duck shapes
    - Scissors (adult only)
    - Crayons
    - 5 clothespins to stand the ducks up
    - 1 upturned laundry basket or a sofa cushion
    - 1 blue dish towel or scarf for the pond
    - Masking tape
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag]
    - 1 pillowcase or tote bag
    - 6–8 household objects, one per rhyme: a spoon, a card star, a rolled sock, a watch, a plastic cup, an egg cup, one of the cardboard ducks
    - 1 sheet of card and 1 marker for picture labels
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Three objects, one bag, sat on the bed.
    - Line the objects up and let her set the order you sing them in.
    - Hide the five ducks round the room and sing a verse for each one found.
    - Draw the labels and stop there; the bag can wait until tomorrow.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **Feeding the ducks** at the pond, with oats or defrosted peas rather than bread
    - The park, for the swings and a shorter walk home
    - A long loop with the stroller if the pond is frozen over
    - The playground with the tunnel, out of the wind
    - A drive to somewhere with water, and stay in the warm car

    - 1 teaspoon each

    - 1 marker and a strip of paper for the label
  [🌅 Opening Activity: The Back of Her Hand]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 metal spoon · 1 wooden spoon · 1 dish towel · a clear counter.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Coldest Thing in the House]
    - 1 laundry basket
    - 4 dish towels laid out on the rug as the four groups
    - Masking tape, cut into labels, and 1 marker
    - Paper and a pencil for the list
    - 1 metal spoon, carried as the thing everything gets compared against
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Coins That Come Back]
    - 20 or so dull coins, copper ones for preference
    - Ketchup, 2 tablespoons, or 1 tablespoon of salt stirred into 3 tablespoons of vinegar
    - 1 small bowl and 1 old teaspoon
    - 2 rags or a cut-up t-shirt
    - 1 bowl of clean water and a dish towel
    - The forks and spoons out of the drawer
  [🕗 The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes** · 🛋️ Moving the Spot, which is the whole event
    - **The first good hour** · 🫱 Things She Can Do From Sitting, one at a time
    - **When she gets sharp with you** · the energy has run out. Back to the blanket
    - **After the first long sleep** · food, and more of it than yesterday
    - **The middle of the day** · 😴 The Rest Block, and she will fight it
    - **When she asks for food** · say yes to almost all of it
    - **Before the afternoon dip** · one job with a visible end
    - **The end of the day** · an early bath and no plans made out loud for tomorrow
  [🫱 Lap Things]
    - **The Window List** · she names everything that goes past and you write it on the back of an envelope
    - **The Story With Her Name In It** · you make it up as you go, and she chooses what happens next when she has it in her
    - **The Warm Washcloth** · a bowl of warm water on the floor and a cloth she wipes her own face and hands with
    - **Five Minutes on One Page** · one page of a picture book held open that long, hunting for small things in the corners
    - **The Popsicle That Counts as a Drink** · for a raw throat, and it goes on the fluids tally
    - **Every Photo Named Out Loud** · your phone on airplane mode and the camera roll gone through one picture at a time
  [🫱 Things She Can Do From Sitting]
    - **The Drawing She Doesn't Have to Explain** · a hardback book under the paper for a desk, crayons loose on the blanket, and no questions about what it is
    - **The Button Tin on a Tray** · sort them however she likes, tip them out, do it again
    - **The Order Pad** · she writes an order from the blanket however her writing comes out and you fetch it, one item at a time
    - **Rolling It Back** · she rolls a ball across the floor to you from where she sits and you roll it back, for as long as it holds her
    - **Reading to Kreston** · board books held up from the sofa while he sits below her on his blanket
    - **Joseph on Speaker** · him or a grandparent, and she does the talking with the phone flat on her chest
    - **One Sticker Sheet, One Page** · no rules, no theme, and the sheet is hers alone
  [🎲 Alternative Activities]
    - **The Dark by Five Countdown** · she checks the window every fifteen minutes and shouts when the streetlights come on
    - **The Hallway Tow** · she kneels on a bath towel holding the front edge and you pull her from the bedroom door to the kitchen and back
    - **Every Shoe in the House** · lined up biggest to smallest along the hall with yours at the end
    - **The Two-Minute Race** · a timer, one room, and everything that lives somewhere else goes in a basket
    - **The Upside-Down Meal** · dinner food at breakfast time, eaten on a blanket on the floor
    - **The Teddy Toboggan** · send soft toys down the stairs on a cushion and mark whose goes furthest
    - **Three Mugs, Three Temperatures** · cool, warm and warmer, and she picks the one that is right
    - **The Fogged Mirror** · run the hot water, let the mirror steam up and draw in it with a finger
    - **Ten Things You Can Hear From the Sofa** · sit still, eyes shut, and count them off on her fingers
    - **The Reverse Tidy** · you put things in the wrong rooms and she finds all six
    - **The Boot Check** · every pair of boots by the door tried on by the wrong person
  [🎨 Alternative Arts & Crafts]
    - **The Frost Picture** · white crayon on white paper, then a wash of watery blue over the top
    - **The Salt Bloom** · sprinkle table salt into a wet blue wash and watch the blotches open while it dries
    - **The Cotton Swab Snow** · white paint dots on the darkest paper in the house
    - **The Cold Tea Map** · dab cold tea on paper with a cloth, dry it on the radiator, and it comes out old
    - **Fork Prints** · dip the tines in paint and print grass, fences and hair
    - **Bubble Wrap Stamping** · paint on a scrap of packing wrap, press it down, peel it up
    - **The Paper Chain Down the Banister** · cut strips, loop them, and stop when the chain reaches the bottom step
    - **The Cereal Box Sign** · cut the front off, draw on the plain side, and tape it to her bedroom door
    - **The Torn Paper Hill** · no scissors at all, and the ragged edge is the point
    - **The Handprint Bird** · her hand printed sideways, then a beak and a leg added in marker
    - **The Envelope Book** · three envelopes stapled down one side and something small posted into each page
  [👶 Kreston's Turn]
    - **The Muffin Tin of One Thing Each** · a spoon, a lid and a washcloth in separate cups for him to pull out
    - **The Basket He Can Empty** · a low basket of safe objects taken out one at a time, refilled by you, emptied again
    - **The Mirror at Floor Level** · an unbreakable mirror propped against the sofa for him to rock toward
    - **The Cushion Step** · one sofa cushion laid flat with a favorite toy on top, close enough to reach across from sitting

    - The bakery, for the doorway more than the bun
    - The market, if it runs today
    - A loop past the pizza place, the florist and the laundromat, naming each one
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Clearing the Runway]
    **🧰 You need:** the hall, or the longest run of floor in the house · masking tape · 1 envelope and a pen.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Six Stations Down the Hall]
    - The cleared hall, taped at both ends
    - Sofa cushions, 3–4
    - 2 dining chairs
    - 1 blanket or a bath towel
    - 4 books, to weight the blanket corners
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 cardboard box with the flaps cut off, or the laundry basket
    - 1 saucepan lid and 1 wooden spoon
    - The envelope of her ideas
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Judge's Chair]
    - The course, still up from this morning
    - 1 dining chair set at the finish line
    - Paper and a marker for the scorecard
    - 1 dish towel as the flag
    - Everybody in the house
    - The pan lid and the spoon
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Counting the Gusts]
    **🧰 You need:** the window the weather hits hardest · 1 old envelope · 1 pen.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Bangs and What Makes Them]
    - The whole house, one room at a time
    - Paper and a marker for the list
    - 1 old towel, rolled
    - Thick card or a folded cereal box, for wedging
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - 1 heavy book
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pretend Power Cut]
    - 2 flashlights, or one and a phone
    - Blankets, 2, and every cushion in the room
    - 1 pitcher of water and 2 cups
    - The afternoon snack, whatever is in the cupboard that needs no stove
  [🕰️ The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes.** Bring the drink to her and watch how she takes it. Her hands and her voice tell you both more about how she is doing than any question you could ask.
    - **The good stretch, which comes early.** The best hours today land in the morning, so spend them. One job with an end to it, then a book, then stop while she still has some left.
    - **When she asks for food.** Appetite comes back sideways. Offer what she asked for yesterday and was refused, and let her leave most of it.
    - **The rest block after lunch.** Shorter today and it still happens, whatever she says about it.
    - **Before the afternoon dip.** The bath, warm and long, and back into pajamas afterwards even though it is the middle of the day.
    - **The end of the day.** Same bedtime as a well day, and hold it. A child who slept all afternoon yesterday needs the shape back.
  [🎲 Alternative Activities]
    - **The Cupboard Nobody Sorts.** Everything out of one low cupboard, sorted her way, and back in before lunch.
    - **The Weight Line.** Five cans from the pantry, lifted one at a time and lined up lightest to heaviest.
    - **Statues Until the Song Stops.** Music on, everybody dancing, and she runs the pause button.
    - **Hide the Spoon.** One wooden spoon hidden in one room, and warmer or colder called out from the doorway.
    - **The Cushion Island Game.** Every cushion on the floor, and the floor between them counts as water.
    - **The Big Pile of Shoes.** Every shoe in the house tipped out and matched into pairs, biggest to smallest.
    - **Washing the Windows From the Inside.** A spray bottle of water, a cloth each, and her half of the glass is hers.
    - **The Cold Doorstep Minute.** Coats on, sixty seconds on a salted step in the falling snow, back in for something warm.
    - **The Phone Call to Somebody Far Away.** She dials, she talks, and you stay out of the conversation.
    - **The Grocery List She Dictates.** She says what the house needs and you write every item down, including the four cakes.
    - **Everything That Fits in a Shoebox.** A hunt through the house, and a long argument about the things that almost fit.
  [🎨 Alternative Arts & Crafts]
    - **Foil Animals on a Plate.** Kitchen foil squeezed into creatures that have to stand up on their own.
    - **The Junk Mail Chain.** Strips looped and taped end to end until the chain crosses the room.
    - **Two Crayons, Two Hands.** One in each fist on a big sheet, both arms moving at once.
    - **The Sticky Wall.** Plastic wrap taped across a doorway sticky side out, and torn tissue pressed onto it.
    - **The Drawing That Comes Through the Paint.** Heavy crayon first, then a watery wash over the top of it.
    - **Tape Under the Paint.** Masking tape shapes stuck down, painted over, peeled off when dry.
    - **Printing With What Is in the Fridge.** A halved potato, the end of a celery bunch and a fork, pressed into paint.
    - **Soap Drawings on the Mirror.** A dry bar of soap on the bathroom mirror, wiped off with a warm cloth after.
    - **Drawing With Her Feet.** Paper taped to the floor and a fat crayon held between her toes.
    - **The Torn Cloud Sky.** White paper torn into lumps and glued across a page of gray.
    - **The Family Drawn From Behind.** Everybody in the house drawn from the back of the head, and guessed at afterwards.
  [👶 Kreston's Turn]
    - **The Muffin Tin and Four Cloths.** A washcloth pushed into each cup for him to haul back out.
    - **Two Lids and a Loud Floor.** A pan lid in each hand on the kitchen floor, banged together for as long as you can stand it.
    - **The Face That Comes Back.** A dish towel over your head, then off, ten times, and he pulls it the last few.
    - **The Cold Spoon From the Fridge.** A metal teaspoon chilled for ten minutes, his to hold and chew while his gums work.

D91 (Thu Apr 1) Tricks and a Very Long Line
  [⏰ At-a-Glance Schedule]
    - **8:00–9:00 AM** — 🌅 Opening Activity: The Grubby Parade
    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)
    - **9:15–10:15 AM** — 🎨 The Main Event: Dirty End, Clean End (60 min)
    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🏠 Instead of Getting Outside: Ten Trips to the Drying Rack (45 min)
    - **11:15–11:45 AM** — Free Play (30 min)
    - **11:45 AM–12:00 PM** — 🤸 Wiggle Time (15 min)
    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch
    - **12:30–1:00 PM** — 📚 Book Time
    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: Clipped Up in Size Order (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🎯 Afternoon Alternatives (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: Emptying the Rinse Bowl
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: The Cave Under the Kitchen Table
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: The Long Bath, No Hurry
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Five Things Are Wrong]
    **🧰 You need:** the table you set wrong last night · 1 sheet of paper · a crayon.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Three Tricks for Joseph]
    - Paper and crayons
    - Scissors
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - His mug
    - His shoes
    - 1 paper plate
    - A lump of playdough or scrunched paper for the pretend food
    - 1 permanent marker
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Longest Line in the House]
    - Her toys, books and shoes
    - Cans and boxes from the cupboard
    - Masking tape for the start line
    - 1 tape measure or a length of string
    - Your phone
    - 1 laundry basket for putting it back
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Fold the dry washcloths corner to corner and stack them into a tower.
    - Wash the plastic animals a second time in a bowl of bubbles, for the bubbles.
    - Give her a bowl of warm water and a paintbrush and let her paint the kitchen floor tiles until they dry off.
    - Line the clean boots up by the door in size order, every pair in the house.
    - Tape a sheet of paper to the window and trace the rain tracks down it with a crayon.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **The garden center**, and let her choose one packet of seed for the sill
    - The hardware store, to look at the long straight things
    - The park, for running after a morning of standing still
    - The duck pond with a handful of oats
  [🌅 Opening Activity: The Visitor at the Door]
    **🧰 You need:** one soft toy she has not played with in weeks · a paper bag.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Showing Him How It All Works]
    - The visitor
    - 1 sturdy step stool
    - 1 dish towel for spills
    - 1 plastic cup
    - Your worst pretend voice
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bed He Has to Fit In]
    - The visitor
    - 1 length of string, about 3 feet
    - 3 boxes of different sizes from the recycling, one a shoebox
    - 1 roll masking tape
    - Scissors (adult only)
    - 1 washcloth, 1 clean sock, 1 dish towel
    - Crayons
  [🌅 Opening Activity: The Grubby Parade]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 laundry basket · 1 old towel spread on the kitchen floor.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Dirty End, Clean End]
    - 2 dishpans or large mixing bowls
    - Warm water, plus a squirt of dish soap in one bowl
    - 1 nail brush and 1 old toothbrush
    - 1 old towel spread on the floor
    - 1 dish towel for drying
    - 1 step stool
    - An apron, or clothes you have given up on
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Clipped Up in Size Order]
    - 1 drying rack, or a length of string tied between two chair backs
    - 20 clothespins, or binder clips and hair clips if the bag of pins runs short
    - A basket of small clean laundry: washcloths, dish towels, socks, pillowcases
    - The boots and plastic animals she washed this morning
    - 1 marker
  [🕰️ The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes.** Dressed in clothes, not pajamas. It changes how she behaves all day.
    - **After breakfast.** The smallest version of the morning, below. Ten minutes of each thing.
    - **When she flags.** Stop before she asks you to. She will not ask.
    - **The middle of the day.** The rest block, shorter than yesterday and still not skipped.
    - **When she asks for food.** Lunch on the blanket, where she already is.
    - **Late afternoon.** One quiet job with a visible end, then nothing.
    - **Early evening.** Bed at the same time as yesterday, not the usual time.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **The Deepest Puddle on the Street.** She stands a stick in each one and you rank them shallow, deeper, deepest.
    - **Under the Umbrella, Listening.** She sits under an open umbrella in the rain with her eyes shut and names what she hears.
    - **What the Wind Takes First.** Hold up a ribbon, a leaf and a paper towel off the back step and see which one goes.
    - **Boot Prints Down the Hall.** A roll of paper down the hallway and one walk across it in wet boots.
    - **The Warmest Spot in the House.** She hunts for it with the back of her hand and marks the winner with a sticky note.
    - **Ten Things That Rattle.** She shakes her way around the kitchen cabinets and lines up the loudest five.
    - **The First Warm Step.** On the first mild afternoon, shoes off on the back step for two minutes and nothing else.
    - **Counting the Buds on One Branch.** Same branch every few days, and the number climbs all month.
    - **The Race Between Two Raindrops.** Pick two on the window and call the winner at the sill.
    - **Everything That Fits in One Boot.** She loads it, you both count what came out.
    - **The Mud Footprint Guessing Game.** You press a print in the mud with a shoe and she works out whose it is.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **Rain-Spotted Paper.** Chalk dust or marker dots on paper, held out in the rain for five seconds and brought straight back in.
    - **Three Jars of Mud Paint.** Dirt and water at three thicknesses, painted onto a flattened box with a stick.
    - **The Wind Spinner Off a Cereal Box.** A spiral cut from card, hung on thread by the window that always leaks air.
    - **Gray Day Paper Chain.** One strip for every gray day this month, in every gray she can find in the crayon box.
    - **Tissue Paper on a Wet Window.** Colored tissue squares pressed to damp glass, backlit whenever the sun does show up.
    - **The Egg Carton Bulb Tray.** Cups torn off, painted, upended and pushed into a tray as a row of crocuses.
    - **The Puddle Painted From Memory.** Back from the walk, she paints the biggest one she saw, before she looks at a photo.
    - **A Kite That Only Works Indoors.** Ribbons taped to a stick and run down the hall at full speed.
    - **The Sock Puppet With a Cold.** One sock, a marker, a scrap of tissue, and a voice she has heard all week.
    - **Stamping With the Bottom of a Boot.** Paint on the sole, one press per sheet, a whole line of them along the hall.
    - **The Weather Wheel for the Fridge.** A paper circle in quarters, a card arrow, and one turn every morning.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **The Cruising Rail.** Two chairs pushed until their edges overlap, so he can sidestep the whole length with a hand on each.
    - **The Muffin Tin and Six Lids.** One plastic lid dropped into every cup, taken out again, and dropped back in with a thumb and finger.
    - **Puffs on the Tray.** A handful of cereal puffs spread wide, worked at with thumb and finger, all through her main event.
    - **Waving From the Window.** Whoever leaves the house waves from the path and waits there until he waves back.

    - **9:00–9:15 AM** — Morning Meeting (15 min)

    - **10:15–10:30 AM** — Snack (15 min)
    - **10:30–11:15 AM** — 🏠 Instead of Getting Outside: The Warmest Place in the House (45 min)

    - Thin card for the frames
    - String
    - 1 pencil
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - One shelf, three things, three prices, and stop there.
    - Deliver the shopping to every door in the house in the laundry basket.
    - Sort the goods into what you eat and what you wear.
    - Play the whole shop with words and no beans, and see whether she notices.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **The hardware store**, for a bag of gravel and a second jar
    - The garden center, to see what is flowering this week
    - The park, to check whether the bumblebee tree is still busy
    - A walk to deliver a taped flower frame to a neighbor
    - The corner store, and she carries the bag home

    **🧰 You need:** masking tape · 1 marker · 3 slips of paper · her boots by the door.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Three Hundred Steps to the Duck Pond]
    - 1 roll masking tape

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Six Games]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 marker · the two jars and the beans.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Six Games, Two Jars]
    - 2 clean jars, the same size
    - Dried beans or dried pasta, a large handful
    - 1 roll masking tape for the lines
    - 1 laundry basket
    - 2 rolled pairs of socks
    - 1 hardcover book
    - 1 tablespoon and 1 orange, or an onion or a potato if the fruit bowl is empty
    - 1 ball, bigger than her fist
    - The sheet of six games
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Shop That Takes Beans]
    - The beans, in one bowl
    - 8–10 things off the shelves: a can, a box of pasta, a roll of tape, two socks, a book
    - Sticky labels, or scraps of paper and masking tape
    - 1 marker
    - 2 paper bags
    - 1 tray as the counter
    - 2 cups, one for each purse
  [🕗 The Shape of Today]
    - **Whenever she wakes:** dressed, but in soft clothes, and breakfast wherever she wants to eat it.
    - **Through the morning:** one thing at a time with a gap after it, and no second thing until she asks.
    - **When she asks for food:** a proper meal if she wants one, and half of it back in the fridge if she does not.
    - **Before the afternoon dip:** the rest block, and hold this one even though she will fight it.
    - **Late afternoon:** the phone call, then a bath and bed at the usual time.
  [🎲 Alternative Activities]
    - **Naming the Puddles:** every puddle between your door and the corner gets a name and a size word.
    - **The Two-Minute Rain Run:** out in whatever she has on, once around the house, back in, towel over her head.
    - **Barefoot on Four Floors:** carpet, tile, wood and doormat, named with her eyes shut.
    - **The Shoe Line, Biggest to Smallest:** every shoe in the house laid along the hall in one order.
    - **A Ribbon Out the Window:** tied to the latch at breakfast and checked again at every meal.
    - **The Stairs, Counted Both Ways:** counting up on the way up, counting back down on the way down.
    - **One Thing in a Sock:** one object dropped into a long sock, named by hand before it comes out.
    - **Everything Green She Can Carry:** one pile in the middle of the floor, then all of it back where it came from.
    - **The Windowsill Cafe:** two cups, two chairs at the glass, and whatever goes past.
    - **The Drawer of Spoons:** tipped onto a dish towel and sorted back into families.
    - **Warm Hands on Cold Glass:** hands in warm water, then flat on the window, and the print that comes up.
  [🎨 Alternative Arts & Crafts]
    - **Blowing the Paint Across the Page:** a puddle of watery paint, a drinking straw, and how far she can push it.
    - **A Nest for the Ones Outside:** yarn scraps, dryer lint and short bits of string on a saucer by the step.
    - **Printing With the Bottom of a Boot:** paint on the sole, then walk it across a long sheet of paper.
    - **The Longest Snake in the House:** playdough rolled thin until it runs off the end of the table.
    - **Two Tubes and a Piece of String:** binoculars for the street, colored in before they get used.
    - **The Drawing You Both Work On:** one sheet, a crayon each, and nobody says what it is going to be.
    - **Newspaper Hats for Everybody:** one fold each, and a name on every brim.
    - **Foil Pressed Flat:** a smooth sheet and a blunt pencil, and every mark she makes stays there.
    - **Blue and White and Nothing Else:** one painting with two colors and no others on the table.
    - **The Card That Goes in the Mailbox:** her drawing, your words underneath, walked to the box and posted.
    - **A Picture With No Brush:** a sponge, a cotton ball and both thumbs.
  [👶 Kreston's Turn]
    - **The Cruising Lane:** two chairs pushed together, backs out, so he sidesteps the whole run with a hand on each.
    - **In and Out of the Big Pot:** a saucepan and four things too big to swallow, taken out and put back for as long as it holds him.
    - **The Point-and-Name Lap:** carried around the room, with a word for whatever his finger lands on, including the fourth time.
    - **Paper He Is Allowed to Wreck:** one whole newspaper page, too big to get in his mouth, and the noise is the whole point.

    **🧰 You need:** 1 orange or a tangerine · 1 plate.
  [🧺 Ten Things in the Wrong Room]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 laundry basket · your legs.

    **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 marker · the fridge.
  [🎲 Alternative Activities]
    - **The Storm Tally** — one mark on the door frame paper per rumble, read out at dinner.
    - **Dandelion Clocks** — one puff each, then count what is left on the stalk.
    - **After-Dinner Walk** — the long May light spent on a slow lap of the block in pajamas.
    - **Barefoot on Three Floors** — grass, tile and carpet, and her verdict on each one.
    - **Maple Helicopters** — two dropped off the step at once and she calls the winner.
    - **The Hose With Her Thumb On It** — ten minutes of watering, and her thumb doing the work.
    - **Ants at the Crack** — one crumb left on the path and checked every half hour.
    - **The Sound Under the Umbrella** — standing still in the rain to listen to it hit.
    - **The Wet Grass Sprint** — shoes off, fence to door and back twice, feet dried on the mat.
    - **The Stairs Counted Two Ways** — numbers going up, the same numbers backward coming down.
    - **The Cupboard Lunch** — one shelf, and she picks the whole meal off it.
  [🎨 Alternative Arts & Crafts]
    - **The Dandelion Rub** — yellow heads rubbed hard on paper until the color comes off.
    - **The Grass Brush** — a fistful of grass taped to a stick and used as a paintbrush.
    - **Tape Resist Letters** — her initial taped on paper, painted over, the tape peeled off last.
    - **The Egg Carton Caterpillar** — six cups, twelve legs and a name she picks.
    - **Petal Prints** — petals folded inside paper and tapped with the back of a spoon.
    - **Cardboard Sunglasses** — cut from a cereal box with colored plastic wrap for lenses.
    - **String Painting** — a paint-soaked string dragged out from between two folded sheets.
    - **The Foil Flower** — foil pressed around a bottle cap and bent out into petals.
    - **The Junk Mail Collage** — flyers and envelopes torn up and glued into one shape.
    - **Handprint Wings** — two hands printed either side of her name down the middle.
    - **Wax Rain** — white crayon lines pressed hard, then blue paint washed over the top.
  [👶 Kreston's Turn]
    - **The Low Shelf He Can Empty** — one shelf cleared and refilled with things he may pull out and drop.
    - **Cruising for a Cracker** — the snack set at the far end of the sofa, with the furniture in between.
    - **One Word All Day** — a single object named the same way every time it comes near him.
    - **The Roasting Pan of Warm Water** — two inches of water on a towel, one cup, and you within arm's reach.

    - 1 teddy or plastic doll that can get wet
    - 2 old towels
    - 1 marker
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Starfish Outline]

    - A flat patch of patio or path

    - 1 kitchen timer or a phone timer
    - 2 towels
    - 1 washcloth
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Chalk starfish only, no pool, and float on the lawn.
    - Race the teddies with their bottles still taped on.
    - The bowl on a towel indoors, faces in the water only.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **Meet a friend at the park**, and take the chalk
    - The splash pad, if your town runs one by mid-June
    - The shady end of the green, if the sun is too much
    - A walk for popsicles, eaten on a wall

    - Paper tickets she cuts herself

    - The lifeguard chair indoors, watching over a bowl of water.
    - Teddy swimming lessons in the bath instead.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **A sunset walk**, the afternoon version of it, once the paving has cooled
    - The river path, for water she cannot get into
    - The park with the shade trees and a picnic blanket
    - The garden with the sprinkler on and nowhere to be

    - **12:00–12:30 PM** — Lunch

    - **1:00–2:30 PM** — 🎨 Second Main Event: The Red Picnic (90 min)
    - **2:30–3:30 PM** — 🌳 Out Again: **Pick one** (60 min)
    - **3:30–4:00 PM** — Snack & Free Play
    - **4:00–4:15 PM** — 🧹 Her Job: Rinsing the Strawberries
    - **4:15–5:00 PM** — Quiet Play: Up on Your Hip Along the Line
    - **5:00–5:45 PM** — 🛁 Wind-Down: A Cool Bath and Two Books
    - **6:00 PM** — **Dinner**
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Grown-Ups Who Used to Be Babies]
    **🧰 You need:** your phone · 1 sheet of paper · 1 pencil.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Who Was Here First]
    - String or wool, 3 meters
    - 8–10 clothespins
    - Card cut from a cereal box, 1 piece per person
    - Crayons and 1 marker
    - Your phone, for photos to copy from
    - 1 roll masking tape
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Red Picnic]
    - Strawberries, 1 punnet
    - Watermelon, 1 wedge
    - Cherry tomatoes, 1 handful, quartered lengthways, and 1 red apple
    - Red fruit punch, or 2 hibiscus tea bags cooled and sweetened (cranberry juice substitutes for either)
    - 1 mug, for steeping
    - Paper cups and teaspoons, for freezing
    - 2 trays, the second one Kreston's
    - 1 table knife (**adult only**)
  [🎯 Afternoon Alternatives]
    - Red fruit straight out of the punnet, no tray, no arranging.
    - Paint with a squashed strawberry instead of eating it.
    - Freeze the drink in an ice cube tray and drop the cubes into water.
    - Lay the tray out in stripes and eat one stripe at a time.
  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]
    - **The aquarium**, dark the whole way round and out of the heat for an hour
    - The kiddie pool at home, if nobody can face the car
    - A shaded walk by the water, late enough that the path has cooled
    - The library, for the air conditioning as much as the books
    - A friend's garden, with the rest of the strawberries

    - **Whenever she wakes**, downstairs in pajamas, and say out loud that today is another indoor day so it stops being a negotiation.
    - **The first stretch**, the jar, which takes as long as it takes.
    - **When she asks for food**, more than yesterday and less than she thinks.
    - **The long middle**, the rest block, shorter today and still the center of it.
    - **After the rest**, the bag of things she has not seen since March.
    - **Before the afternoon dip**, the list, dictated from where she is sitting.
    - **The end of the afternoon**, a window open, a blanket, and a look at the street from indoors.
    - **Bed at the usual time**, because tomorrow is the day she goes back out.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **Barefoot on Six Surfaces**, grass, paving, doormat, tile, carpet and gravel, named as she crosses each one.
    - **One Hand Cold, One Hand Warm**, two bowls and a third in the middle that both hands go into at the end.
    - **Melting an Ice Cube in Her Fist**, one cube each, and the first hand to come up empty wins.
    - **The Hallway Bowling Alley**, six empty bottles at one end and a soft ball at the other.
    - **The Upside-Down Afternoon**, dinner food at four and pajamas straight after it.
    - **Hide the Timer**, wound up, hidden in one room, and found by the ticking.
    - **The Great Shoe Sort**, every pair in the house paired up and lined out by size along the hall.
    - **Counting the Steps to the Corner**, walked out at eight in the morning before the heat arrives.
    - **The Watering Can Round**, every pot in the yard done before the sun gets on them.
    - **The Fridge Inventory**, she names everything on one shelf out loud while you write it down.
    - **Grandma on Speakerphone**, she does the talking and you stay out of it.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **The Paper Bag Cut Flat**, a bigger sheet than any she owns and crayons in the middle of it.
    - **Chalk on the Shady Wall**, one wall she is allowed to draw on and a bucket of water to take it off.
    - **Painting the Path With Water**, one wide brush and a bucket, and the whole thing gone by lunch.
    - **The Sticker Made of Tape**, a small drawing on a strip of masking tape, stuck where she says.
    - **A Crown From a Cereal Box**, cut to fit her head and taped at the back.
    - **Fingerprint Bugs**, one thumb, washable paint, and two legs drawn on each.
    - **The Straw Necklace**, drinking straws cut into lengths and threaded onto wool, up out of reach when she finishes.
    - **A Cover for the Book She Loves**, brown paper, her title, her name.
    - **The Picture Torn, Not Cut**, ripped into six and glued back down in the wrong order.
    - **Painting the Window From Inside**, washable paint on the glass and a cloth to clear it after.
    - **Tape Roads on the Floor**, masking tape from room to room and every wheeled toy in the house on them.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **Carrying One Thing to One Person**, handed over, taken back, and run again twenty times.
    - **The Cup With Two Swallows In It**, offered on the hour and refilled small, because he holds it himself now.
    - **Washcloths Into the Big Bowl**, posted in, tipped out, and nothing in the game small enough to swallow.
    - **The Laundry Basket He Pushes**, upturned on the grass to lean on, where a landing is soft.

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Lining Up Everything That Holds Cold Water]
    **🧰 You need:** every pitcher, bottle, jar and lidded cup in the kitchen · her two hands.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Pitcher That Has to Be Empty by Dinner]
    - 1 large pitcher
    - 1 roll masking tape and 1 permanent marker
    - 3 clear glasses
    - 1 lemon, or any fruit in the bowl, or a spoonful of jam out of the pantry
    - 1 cup per person, and a lidded cup for Kreston
    - 1 tray
    - The kitchen clock
  [🎨 Second Main Event: The Coolest Clothes in the House]
    - The clothes drawers and the closet, hers and yours
    - 1 old sheet, a scarf and a dish towel
    - 1 mirror, or the biggest one you can carry into the room
    - 1 laundry basket
    - Sun hats, both of them
    - Your phone, for the photographs
  [🕰️ The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes.** Dressed if she wants to be, pajamas if she does not. Both are fine.
    - **The first stretch.** One thing with an end to it, done on a lap or a sofa.
    - **When she asks for food.** She is hungry again today, and small and often still beats a plate.
    - **The rest block.** Shorter than yesterday and it still happens, whatever she says about it.
    - **After the rest.** The one hour in the day with any energy in it. Spend it on the round.
    - **Before the afternoon dip.** Everything winds down from three, because she has half a tank.
    - **The end of the day.** Early bath, early bed, and no negotiating over either.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **One Cube in Each Hand.** Two ice cubes, one per fist, and the first hand to come up empty wins.
    - **The Coldest Thing in the Fridge.** The back of her hand on every shelf and every jar, ranked coldest to warmest.
    - **The Full Cup Down the Hall.** A cup filled to the brim carried the length of the hall and back without a drop.
    - **The Smell She Has to Find.** One open jar hidden in the kitchen, and she walks toward it with her eyes shut.
    - **The Shadow Marked Every Hour.** A strip of tape laid where the sunlight edge falls, once an hour, and the strips counted at dinner.
    - **Ten Things That Fit in a Shoe.** She hunts them down and then argues for the eleventh.
    - **The Tape Trail.** Masking tape stuck down as a path around the furniture, walked heel to toe.
    - **The Paper Strip at the Doorway.** A torn strip of newspaper held up in every doorway to find where the air is moving.
    - **Everybody Swaps Places.** Lunch eaten in somebody else's seat, yours included.
    - **The Rice Tray on the Floor.** Two cups, a spoon and a funnel on a towel, in the shade, swept up after.
    - **The Photograph She Explains.** One old picture and ten questions about who is in it and where they were.
    - **Reading in the Empty Bathtub.** Cushion in, water off, two books and the coolest seat in the house.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **Crayon Shavings in the Window.** Shavings on paper on a sunny sill, watched until they slump.
    - **The Paper That Fades.** Keys and a spoon laid on colored paper in the sun for a day, then lifted off.
    - **Foil From One Square.** One square of foil, one animal, no tape and no scissors.
    - **The Cardboard Fan.** A cereal box cut into a paddle, drawn on by her, waved at whoever is hottest.
    - **Newspaper Hats.** One each, then a row of small ones for the animals.
    - **Junk Mail Confetti.** Torn small, shaken in a jar, poured out on a dark plate and sorted by color.
    - **The Envelope Zoo.** Old envelope corners cut into mouths, drawn on, fed paper scraps.
    - **The Long Strip Family.** Everyone in the house drawn end to end on a strip as long as the table.
    - **Bottle Cap Printing.** Caps dipped in paint, printed in rows, counted when they dry.
    - **Paper Plate on Her Head.** She draws a face on a plate held on top of her head, then looks at what she made.
    - **Chalk on Dark Paper.** A flattened cereal box colored dark, chalk over the top, smudged with a thumb.
    - **The Flip Book.** Ten pages of a stick figure on the corner of an old notepad, thumbed through fast.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **The Basket That Goes to the Other Room.** Three soft things in, carried through, tipped out, carried back.
    - **One Step, Then Down Again.** The bottom stair or a firm cushion, up and down with one hand held, twenty times.
    - **Posting the Big Lids.** Palm-sized lids through a slot cut in a shoebox, and nothing smaller than his fist.
    - **The Wet Sponge and the Empty Bowl.** An inch of water in one bowl, a sponge, an empty bowl to fill, and you within arm's reach of both.

  [🕗 The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes.** Bigger breakfast than yesterday, and let her ask for it rather than offering it.
    - **The first good hour.** Take it. The ice tray on her lap, and the whole morning can be that.
    - **When she starts wandering.** She is done, whatever she says. Wind it down before she falls apart.
    - **After lunch.** The rest block, and it happens whether she believes in it or not.
    - **The long afternoon.** The window list, drinks, and one thing she chooses.
    - **Before dinner.** Bath, early bed, and tomorrow still counts as a recovery day.
  [🙂 Once She Has Settled: The Five Faces on the Envelope]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 envelope or the back of a cereal box · 1 marker.
  [🎯 Four Things That Work Lying Down]
    - Naming everything she can see from where her head is, ceiling first.
    - One hand in a bowl of cool water on a towel on your lap.
    - Your phone on the arm of the sofa, playing the songs she picks.
    - A cold washcloth over her forehead, taken off and put back by her.
  [🧊 Whenever She Wakes: The Ice That Melts in Her Hand]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 block of ice turned out of a cup · 1 shallow bowl · 1 tray · 1 dish towel · 1 teaspoon of salt · 2 drops of food coloring · 1 small pitcher of warm water.
  [🪟 The Long Afternoon: The List of Everything Out the Window]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 pen · the sofa nearest a window.
  [🎯 Four Things She Can Do From the Sofa]
    - Sorting a bowl of clean socks into pairs on her stomach.
    - A flashlight and the ceiling, with the blinds down.
    - Telling you what to draw while you hold the paper up.
    - One tray of dry crackers she is allowed to eat crumb by crumb.
  [🎲 Alternative Activities]
    - **The Cicada Shell Hunt**, along tree trunks and fence posts at her head height, lined up on a plate at the end.
    - **The Fridge Picnic**, everything cold in there on one tray, eaten sitting on the kitchen floor.
    - **Half a Lemon Each**, squeezed by hand into cold water with as much sugar as she argues for.
    - **The Bug Under the Glass**, a clean jar set over something on the path, a good look, then a lift and a wave.
    - **Breakfast Outside at Seven**, the table carried out while the concrete is still cool and nobody has dressed.
    - **The Basket of Warm Laundry**, tipped onto the floor for her to find every pair in it.
    - **Reading in the Bathtub**, an inch of cool water, no soap, two books that can take a splash.
    - **The Nap Under the Table**, pillows pushed underneath and the fan pointed at the gap.
    - **Counting the Cars by Color**, from the shaded step, one tally mark each on the back of an envelope.
    - **The Long Grass Barefoot**, shoes off on the shaded lawn at six, naming what her feet find.
    - **The Cold Bag Race**, unpacking the groceries with the freezer things put away first and fast.
  [🎨 Alternative Arts & Crafts]
    - **Sun Prints on Colored Paper**, leaves laid out on a sunny sill and lifted two days later to find their shadows.
    - **The Paper Bag Sun Hat**, folded, taped to fit her head and drawn all over.
    - **Sidewalk Paint**, cornstarch, water and food coloring in muffin cups, brushed on thick and hosed off at five.
    - **The Corn Husk Doll**, husks off the cobs from dinner, twisted and tied with yarn.
    - **The Torn Paper Sunset**, magazine strips torn along their length and glued down in bands.
    - **Seed Mosaic on a Paper Plate**, melon and sunflower seeds saved from the week and glued in rings.
    - **The Pressed Flower Card**, whatever is out in the yard flattened under the heaviest book in the house.
    - **Flyswatter Painting**, a spoonful of paint on a tray, one clean swatter and a sheet taped to the fence.
    - **The Foil Sun Catcher**, a strip of foil scrunched and smoothed again, hung where the light hits it.
    - **Chalk Dust on Wet Paper**, a sheet soaked in the sink and chalk dragged across it while it drips.
    - **The Cicada Shells on a Card**, the morning's finds glued in a row with a word from her under each one.
  [👶 Kreston's Turn]

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Rubbing In the Butter]

  [🌳 Out Again: **Pick one**]

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Wet Hands on the Dark Tray]

  [🎨 The Main Event: Where the Water Comes Back]

    - 1 heatproof mug
    - 1 dinner plate wide enough to cover the mug
    - 2 saucers
    - 4–6 ice cubes
    - Masking tape and a marker
    - 1 wide paintbrush
    - 1 dish towel
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Washing Day at the Kitchen Sink]
    - A sturdy chair or step stool at the sink
    - Dish soap
    - 8–10 small washable things: doll clothes, her socks, washcloths, dish towels
    - 1 dry towel for the floor
    - 1 dry towel for wringing
    - A length of string, 6 feet
    - 8 clothespins
    - An apron or an old shirt
  [🌅 Opening Activity: Four Guesses Taped to the Glass]
    **🧰 You need:** 4 strips of paper · masking tape · 1 marker.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Everything in the Drawer Into One Pot]
    - The biggest pot in the house
    - Whatever the vegetable drawer holds: an onion, 2 carrots, 2 potatoes, a stick of celery, anything going soft. A can of mixed vegetables or a bag of frozen ones stands in for a thin drawer, and a cup of dry lentils or rice fills out a thin pot
    - 1 can of beans or tomatoes
    - 1 bouillon cube, a spoon of bouillon, or salted water
    - 1 butter knife (hers) and 1 sharp knife (**adult only**)
    - 2 chopping boards
    - 1 wooden spoon
    - A bowl for her cut pieces
    - Half a loaf of bread, or a sleeve of crackers
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Eight Things and a Card Each]
    - 1 cloth or dish towel to cover the table
    - 8 objects from around the house, hers to choose
    - Paper torn into 8 rectangles
    - 1 marker
    - Masking tape
    - 1 strip of paper for the sign
    - A phone for photographs
  [🕰️ The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes:** earlier than you want, and asking for things
    - **The first long stretch:** four piles on the blanket, done lying down
    - **When she asks for food:** real food today, small portions, and let her ask twice
    - **The rest block:** the argument of the day, and worth having
    - **After the rest:** one job she can do from the sofa, and Kreston for fifteen supervised minutes
    - **Before the afternoon dip:** the window open a hand's width for four minutes of cold air, from under the blanket
    - **The long evening:** an ordinary bedtime, held firm, because tomorrow is a real day
  [✋ Things You Can Do With One Hand]
    - Draw a shape on her back with one finger and let her guess it.
    - Scroll the photos on your phone, oldest first, while she names who is in them.
    - Tell a story where she gives you one word and you have to get it in.
    - Put one of three objects into her closed hand under the blanket and let her name it.
    - Read the same book four times without mentioning that it is the same book.
    - Say what you are doing in the kitchen out loud so she can hear you from the sofa.
  [✋ More That Works From Horizontal]
    - Read her the shopping list and let her add three things.
    - Put the radio on and let her call out every time she hears a word she knows.
    - Give her the stack of cards from the drawer and let her deal everyone a hand.
    - Line the edge of the blanket with anything from the kitchen drawer and let her arrange it by size.
    - Let her plan tomorrow out loud while you write it down as a list.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **The Coat Argument, Settled at the Corner**, she picks her own coat, you walk two minutes, and the cold decides it.
    - **Six Bottles Down the Hallway**, bowling with the fattest buckeye in the bowl and a rebuilt set every time.
    - **Puddle Depth Testing**, one stick with three stripes on it, and she calls which puddle wins.
    - **The Seed Envelopes**, dry seed heads picked off the stems into labeled envelopes for spring.
    - **The Change Jar Towers**, every coin tipped out, sorted by size, and stacked until they fall.
    - **The Bench Count**, twenty minutes at the bus stop with a tally of what goes each way.
    - **Warm Drink Service**, three mugs, three things to stir in, and she takes the orders.
    - **The Streetlight Mark**, one pencil line on the door frame each week for when the light comes on.
    - **The Apple That Rolls Furthest**, a board propped on a chair, four windfalls, one finish line.
    - **Everything Gray**, a bag walk that takes gray things only, laid out on the windowsill after.
    - **The Preschool Bag Audit**, everything out of the bag she carries on Monday, named, and put back.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **The Paper Bag Owl**, one bag, two circles, a folded beak, ten minutes start to finish.
    - **Leaves Under the Heaviest Books**, pressed on Monday, opened Friday, taped along the window.
    - **The Reply She Waits For**, a cereal box cut to a postcard, drawn on, addressed, mailed, and marked on the calendar.
    - **Torn Paper Trees**, no scissors, a brown trunk and a fistful of colored scraps glued on.
    - **Paper Blotting**, thick paint dotted on one sheet, a second sheet pressed on top, and the two pulled apart.
    - **Buckeye Faces**, a marker, a bowl of buckeyes, and a family of them along the sill.
    - **The Yarn-Wrapped Twig**, one forked stick and a ball of wool wound until it runs out.
    - **The Paper Plate Weather Wheel**, four quarters, a fastener, and she turns it every morning.
    - **Coffee Filter Leaves**, felt tip on the filter, one drip of water, and the color walks outward.
    - **Handprint Bats**, black paper, white paint, and a row of them up the stairs for October.
    - **The Card That Stands Up**, one fold, two cuts, and a mouth that opens when she opens it.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **Two Bowls and One Spoon**, dry cereal moved from bowl to bowl, and most of it lands.
    - **The Naming Round**, a picture book on your knee, and he points forty times while you name.
    - **The Hallway Sprint**, a clear run, a cushion at each end, and he does it until he drops.
    - **The Boots He Fills**, a pair of rain boots and a handful of his own socks to drop down inside.

  [🕗 The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes** — A Different Room to Be Ill In
    - **Before she asks for it** — the cup filled, the blanket where she can kick it off
    - **The first stretch** — The Box for Next Time
    - **When she flags** — the rest block, and she will argue about it
    - **After the first long sleep** — Telling Somebody on the Phone
    - **When she asks for food** — whatever she names, in a smaller amount than she asks for
    - **The afternoon dip** — the rest block again, shorter, eyes open allowed
    - **The long stretch before dinner** — the tray on her knees and no plan for it
    - **When the light goes** — bed at the usual time, however much she slept
  [🛋️ Whenever She Wakes: The Nest You Build Around Her]
    **🧰 You need:** the sofa · 2 pillows · the blanket she asks for · 1 lidded cup · 1 bowl · 1 towel.
  [🎯 If She Wants Something Else]
    - Every crayon in the house tipped onto a tray and sorted into a jar of stubs and a box of good ones.
    - The photographs on your phone from her birthday, named one at a time.
    - A card for whoever she is missing, drawn on her knees and mailed this week.
    - Her own bed made up on the sofa by her, with you handing her the pillows.
  [🛋️ Whenever She Wakes: A Different Room to Be Ill In]
    **🧰 You need:** her blanket · 2 pillows · 1 lidded cup · one thing she has not had out in a week.
  [📦 The First Stretch: The Box for Next Time]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 shoebox with a lid · 1 marker · paper and crayons · whatever is already inside the nest.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **The Draft Hunt** — a strip of paper towel held along every door and window to show where the wind is getting in.
    - **Hands on Everything Cold** — flat palms on the window, the fridge door, the floor and the radiator, ranked coldest to warmest.
    - **Lamps On Before Dinner** — she walks the house switching every light on as the gray comes down and picks the room she likes lit best.
    - **One Branch, Counted** — count what is still hanging on one branch from the window, then count the same branch tomorrow.
    - **Wettest to Driest** — every pair of shoes in the house lined up along the hall in order.
    - **The Mail on the Mat** — the day's junk mail split into a pile to keep, a pile for the recycling and a pile she may tear up.
    - **The Same Three Puddles** — the same short walk two days running, checking whether each puddle grew or went.
    - **Everybody's Coat Pockets** — turned out onto the table, and she works out whose coat is whose from what falls out.
    - **Three Cups, One Toy** — a toy hidden under one of three cups, shuffled on the table, and she keeps her eye on it.
    - **The Ten-Minute Race** — the living room floor cleared before the stove timer goes, both of you working.
    - **Breakfast for Dinner** — eggs and toast at six on a day that got away from everybody.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **The Wet Sky** — clean water brushed over the whole sheet first, then color dropped in to run where it wants.
    - **Pale Crayon on Dark Paper** — the street after dinner, lit windows drawn in white and yellow on a dark sheet.
    - **Junk Mail, One Color Only** — every scrap of one color torn out of the pile and glued into a single block.
    - **The Potato Stamp** — half a potato cut into a shape by you, dipped in paint and pressed until the page is full.
    - **Drawing the Rain** — a whole page of nothing but the lines it makes coming down the glass.
    - **The Paper Bag Face** — a face drawn on a lunch bag that fits over her hand and gets a voice.
    - **Foil Over Something Rough** — aluminum foil pressed down onto a doormat, a brick or a coin until the pattern comes through.
    - **The Torn Tree** — a brown paper trunk, torn scraps for the few leaves left up, and the rest of the scraps on the ground.
    - **Painting With What Isn't a Brush** — a fork, a cork, a crumpled paper towel, a leaf off the step.
    - **Salt on the Wet Sky** — a pinch of table salt dropped onto the wet wash from two lines up and left to dry into blotches.
    - **A Card for Whoever Is Ill** — folded, drawn, and walked around or dropped in the mailbox this week.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **Four Blocks, Then Yours** — he stacks his four and knocks them down, and you add a fifth for him to take off the top.
    - **The Cloth That Is His** — a damp cloth of his own while you wipe the table, and he follows you around the whole countertop.
    - **One Word More Than He Says** — he says *dog*, you say *a wet dog*, and then you wait for him to have a go.
    - **Loading the Machine** — one thing at a time out of the basket and into the washing machine, with your hand on the door.

  [🌅 Opening Activity: The Thumb Test]
    **🧰 You need:** 6 things out of the fridge and the cupboard, 4 of them soft enough to take a thumbprint (mushroom, zucchini, banana, a ripe pear, canned peach halves, a slice of bread) and 2 that will not (onion, carrot, potato, a hard apple) · 1 tray.
  [🎨 The Main Event: The Bridge and the Claw]
    - 1 small serrated table knife with a rounded tip (**adult supervision the whole time**)
    - 1 cutting board and 1 damp dish towel to sit it on
    - Her pile, the four that took a thumbprint
    - Your pile, the two that did not (**yours**)
    - 1 large bowl
    - 1 heavy pot with a lid
    - 1 can of tomatoes, or a jar of pasta sauce, or two spoons of tomato paste and an extra cup of water
    - Oil, salt, 1 cup of water or stock
    - 1 can of beans or a cup of dried lentils, if the six things looked thin in the bowl
  [🎨 Second Main Event: What's Inside the Broken Thing]
    - 1 dead battery-powered object: a radio, a flashlight, a toy that stopped working, a wind-up clock
    - 1 medium Phillips screwdriver and 1 small flathead
    - 1 muffin tin or egg carton, for the screws
    - 1 old towel and 1 tray
    - 1 zip-top bag
    - 1 piece of card, 1 marker, masking tape
  [🕗 The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes.** She will be brighter than yesterday and want the whole day at once. Slow it down before it starts.
    - **After breakfast.** Four spoons, four words, and a small honest taste of food.
    - **When she starts asking for jobs.** One drawer on the blanket, and it can stop after two minutes.
    - **The rest block.** The sleep she says she does not need, and she still needs it.
    - **Before the afternoon dip.** The list for when she is better, dictated by her.
    - **When the light goes.** Warm water and a washcloth where she is lying, then a longer wind-down than usual.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **Fifty Steps Around the House**, counted out loud, then fifty back the other way.
    - **The Mug She Carries Herself**, she picks it, stirs the warm milk and walks it to her own chair.
    - **The Radiator Race**, wet mittens and hats laid out and checked to see which dries first.
    - **Everything Blue in Four Minutes**, a timed hunt with a laundry basket and no rules about what counts.
    - **The Chair Tunnel**, four dining chairs in a row under a blanket, with a job waiting at each end.
    - **The Bell in the Next Room**, eyes shut, and she points at where the sound came from.
    - **Two Minutes of Standing Still**, a contest nobody wins and everybody enters.
    - **The Sofa Cushion Wall**, every cushion off the furniture and built into a wall across one doorway.
    - **The Long Roll Down the Hallway**, a ball pushed back and forth sitting down, legs apart.
    - **The Sock Skate Race**, socks on the smooth floor and the length of the kitchen crossed sliding.
    - **The Newspaper Mountain**, a whole newspaper torn into strips and stuffed into a pillowcase.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **Rain Prints**, a sheet of paper held out under a drip for ten seconds and dried flat.
    - **The Window Sign for the Street**, one big word drawn thick and taped facing out for whoever walks past.
    - **Bubble Wrap Printing**, paint rolled over the bubbles and pressed onto paper.
    - **The Foil Puddle**, foil pressed into a saucer and flooded with watered-down color.
    - **Coffee Grounds Mud Paint**, used grounds stirred into glue for a brown that smells.
    - **The Torn Paper Tree**, brown strips for a trunk and bare branches, because that is what is out there.
    - **Candle Stub Lines**, wax scribbles that the paint refuses to cover.
    - **The Odd Sock Draft Stopper**, stuffed with the rest of the odd socks and given a face.
    - **Cotton Swab Dots**, one color per swab and no brushes allowed.
    - **The Envelope Street**, old envelopes cut into lit windows and glued along a dark sheet.
    - **Potato Stamps**, cut by you and printed by her onto a paper bag for wrapping.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **The Ball Down the Hallway**, you sitting at one end, him kicking from the other.
    - **Two Words at the Window**, *car gone* and *rain wet*, handed back to him each time he points.
    - **Two Cups and the Clothespins**, dropped from one cup into the other and tipped out to start again.
    - **The Pan Lid Drum**, one wooden spoon and one lid, at floor level, for as long as you can stand it.

  [🌅 Opening Activity: Which Floors Slide]
    **🧰 You need:** 2 old towels · thick socks · every floor surface in the house.
  [🎨 The Main Event: Polishing the Hall on Two Towels]
    - 4 old towels, two dry and two damp
    - Thick socks, hers and yours
    - 1 spray bottle of water, or a bowl to dip a corner in
    - Masking tape for the start line, or a folded dish towel laid across it
    - 1 empty laundry basket as the turning post
    - A clear stretch of hard floor, nothing underfoot
  [🎨 Second Main Event: Everybody's Feet in a Row]
    - Flattened cardboard, cereal boxes or a roll of paper, enough for six outlines
    - 1 pencil and thick markers
    - Blunt scissors for her, heavy scissors for you (**adult only**)
    - Masking tape
    - Every pair of shoes and boots by the door, plus the summer ones from the closet
  [⏳ The Shape of the Day]
    - **Whenever she wakes** · she will be up before you want her to be. Sofa, water, and no expectations for an hour.
    - **Once the first hour is behind you** · one thing on the lap tray, sitting up, twenty minutes at most.
    - **When she asks for food** · say yes to whatever she names. Appetite coming back is the news of the day.
    - **Before the afternoon dip** · the rest block, and hold the line on it.
    - **After the rest block** · the one small job, which is the whole point of today.
    - **When it goes dark at four thirty** · lamps low, and no visitors.
    - **Bedtime** · earlier than she thinks she needs, because tomorrow is the day she overdoes it.
  [🛋️ Building It Where She Points]
    **🧰 You need:** every cushion and blanket in the house · her voice · your arms.
  [✏️ The Picture She Orders Up]
    **🧰 You need:** paper on a book · 1 pencil or crayon · her lying flat.
  [📖 The Story You Get Wrong]
    **🧰 You need:** a story she knows by heart · her eyes shut.
  [🎯 If She Will Not Settle]
    - Move the whole nest to your bed and start again there.
    - One long audiobook or a familiar movie, with you on the sofa beside her.
    - A cool washcloth on the back of her neck and the window cracked for one minute.
    - Name every animal she can think of, out loud, taking turns until she stops.
  [🍋 One Sour Thing at a Time]
    **🧰 You need:** a clementine · a lemon wedge · plain yogurt · a pickle or a spoon of cranberry sauce · 1 saucer. With no citrus in the house, an apple wedge and a cracker with a drop of vinegar on it do the same job.
  [🍊 Peeling for the Whole House]
    **🧰 You need:** 4 or 5 clementines, or any fruit in the house with a skin she can get off · 1 bowl for the peel · her lap.
  [📝 The Card That Says What's Next]
    **🧰 You need:** 1 index card or a piece of thick paper · 1 marker · her ideas.
  [🎯 If She Is Climbing the Walls]
    - Sofa to the window and twenty minutes of naming what goes past.
    - Everything in one drawer emptied onto the tray and put back in a new order.
    - A phone call to somebody who will let her do the talking.
    - Ten minutes of her leading and you following, room to room, at her pace.
  [🎲 **Alternative Activities**]
    - **Both Sides of the Front Door** · one hand flat on the inside of the door, one on the storm door glass, and she calls the difference.
    - **Paper Snowball Fight** · a stack of newspaper crumpled into ammunition, the sofa as the wall.
    - **The Practice Wrap** · a shoebox, a sheet of leftover paper and as much tape as she wants to use.
    - **Four O'Clock Lights Off** · every lamp off at the moment it goes dark outside, then one back on at a time.
    - **Guess Which Hand** · a small thing hidden in one fist, three guesses, then she hides and you guess.
    - **The Cupboard Orchestra** · a pan, a wooden spoon and a jar of rice, played loudest to quietest.
    - **The Bed Picnic** · lunch upstairs on a towel, and nobody goes back down until it is finished.
    - **Heavier or Lighter** · two cans, one in each hand, and she calls it before the scale does.
    - **Freeze When It Stops** · three rounds of music and stopping, then she works the button and you freeze.
    - **Everything That Came Out of a Box** · a hunt for what arrived this month, packing paper and all.
    - **The Laundry Basket Boat** · a basket, a cushion and a wooden spoon for an oar.
  [🎨 **Alternative Arts & Crafts**]
    - **Cotton Balls on Blue Paper** · glue and stick, no cutting, snow that stays up till spring.
    - **The Star You Cannot Plan** · paper folded in four, one cut across the corner, and the shape shows up when it opens.
    - **Wrapping Paper Collage** · leftover paper torn small and glued into one big sheet.
    - **The Card for Somebody Far Away** · folded card, her drawing, your envelope and her stamp.
    - **Two Crayons, One Cold** · one crayon out of the freezer and one off the radiator, drawn side by side on the same page.
    - **The Salt Snow Picture** · glue squeezed in lines, salt poured over, the loose stuff tipped off.
    - **Pipe Cleaner People** · bent into a family and stood up on a jar lid.
    - **Buttons Pressed Into Playdough** · the tin emptied out and pushed in row by row.
    - **Circles Without a Brush** · the bottom of a cup dipped in paint and printed across a page.
    - **The Hallway Drawing** · a long strip of paper taped to the wall at her height, added to all week.
    - **A Bag of Snips** · scissors, scrap paper and nothing to make, which is the point.
  [👶 **Kreston's Turn**]
    - **The Bottom Three Stairs** · up and down holding the rail, with you one step below, as many times as he wants.
    - **Feeding the Bear** · a spoon, an empty bowl and a toy that gets a whole meal.
    - **Lids Through the Slot** · a shoebox with a slot cut in the lid and a pile of jar lids to post.
    - **Naming What He Hands You** · he brings things one at a time, you say the word and wait for his version.
